Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).

Installation. Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] fast16 Sabotage Framework Hash IOC Sweep (svcmgmt.exe / fast16.sys / svcmgmt.dll) Installation High
Sweeps endpoint file, process and image-load telemetry for the eight SHA256 hashes published by SentinelLABS for the fast16 sabotage framework — the svcmgmt.exe Lua-VM carrier, the fast16.sys boot-start filesystem driver, and the svcmgmt.dll RAS connotify reporter. High-fidelity because these hashes belong to one disclosed campaign and are not present in benign software inventories. Rationale: Uses the eight SHA256 / SHA1 / MD5 hashes published by SentinelLABS for the disclosed fast16 framework components (svcmgmt.exe carrier, fast16.sys driver, svcmgmt.dll connotify DLL) plus the additional samples in the IOC table. Hashes are immutable per-binary identifiers — any match is high confidence. As of the article's publication (2026-04-23) and 12 days later, no second-source vendor write-up is yet public; ShipShape would normally cite Mandiant/CrowdStrike/MSRC corroboration but here the IOCs come from a single primary disclosure — flag for re-validation once external sources publish.
ATT&CKT1014 T1543.003 T1565.001

union isfuzzy=true
  ( DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or SHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or MD5 in (_fast16_md5)
       or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or InitiatingProcessSHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or InitiatingProcessMD5 in (_fast16_md5)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, EventSource="Process", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessSHA256 ),
  ( DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or SHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or MD5 in (_fast16_md5)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, EventSource="FileWrite", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessSHA256 ),
  ( DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or SHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or MD5 in (_fast16_md5)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, EventSource="ImageLoad", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessSHA256 )
| order by Timestamp desc

union isfuzzy=true
  ( ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or SHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or MD5 in (_fast16_md5)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, EventSource="Process", FileName=TargetProcessName, FilePath=TargetProcessName, SHA256, CommandLine=TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName ),
  ( ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFileSHA256 in (_fast16_sha256) or TargetFileSHA1 in (_fast16_sha1) or TargetFileMD5 in (_fast16_md5)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, EventSource="FileWrite", FileName=TargetFileName, FilePath=TargetFilePath, SHA256=TargetFileSHA256, CommandLine=ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName=ActingProcessName ),
  ( ThreatIntelligenceIndicator
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where FileHashValue in (_fast16_sha256) or FileHashValue in (_fast16_sha1) or FileHashValue in (_fast16_md5)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname="", ActorUsername="", EventSource="TI-Hit", FileName, FilePath="", SHA256=FileHashValue, CommandLine="", ParentProcessName="" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: fast16 Sabotage Framework Binary Execution by Hash
id: 8c4d2e1a-7b3f-4a92-bc56-fa57167a5616
status: experimental
description: Detects execution of binaries belonging to the fast16 sabotage framework disclosed by SentinelLABS — the svcmgmt.exe Lua-VM carrier (2005-08-30 build), the fast16.sys boot-start filesystem driver (2005-07-19 build), and the svcmgmt.dll RAS connotify reporter (2005-06-06 build) — by SHA256 hash. Referenced in the ShadowBrokers Territorial Dispute drv_list.txt deconfliction file.
references:
    - https://www.sentinelone.com/labs/fast16-mystery-shadowbrokers-reference-reveals-high-precision-software-sabotage-5-years-before-stuxnet/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/05

[LLM] fast16 Carrier Runtime Artefacts (SvcMgmt service / pipe p577 / \Device\fast16) Installation Medium
Hunts for the runtime fingerprint left by the fast16 carrier and kernel driver: a Windows service named 'SvcMgmt' pointing at svcmgmt.exe, a named pipe '\\.\pipe\p577' opened by the connotify DLL, the registration of a kernel device '\Device\fast16' / '\??\fast16', and registry persistence at 'PrefetchParameters\EnablePrefetcher=0' performed by the driver entry point. Catches a re-deployed or modernised variant even when hashes have been recompiled. Rationale: Anchored on four article-specific runtime strings that don't appear together by chance: (1) Windows service name 'SvcMgmt' as named in the carrier installer code-path, (2) named pipe '\\.\pipe\p577' decoded by svcmgmt.dll connotify DLL, (3) device object '\Device\fast16' / '\??\fast16' registered by fast16.sys, and (4) the driver's first action of zeroing 'EnablePrefetcher' under PrefetchParameters. Sigma omitted because the detection unions multiple log categories (file_event + registry_event + service install + named pipe). No external corroboration available 12 days post-disclosure.
ATT&CKT1543.003 T1014 T1112 T1564
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry Endpoint.Services
let _fast16_files = dynamic(["svcmgmt.exe","svcmgmt.dll","fast16.sys"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  ( DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
    | where FileName in~ (_fast16_files)
       or FolderPath has @"\Device\fast16"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, EventSource=strcat("File:", ActionType), Detail=strcat(FolderPath, "\\", FileName), SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName ),
  ( DeviceEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType == "NamedPipeEvent"
    | where FileName has "p577" or AdditionalFields has "p577" or AdditionalFields has @"\\.\pipe\p577"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, EventSource="NamedPipe", Detail=tostring(coalesce(FileName, AdditionalFields)), SHA256=InitiatingProcessSHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName ),
  ( DeviceEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType in ("DriverLoad","ServiceInstalled")
    | where FileName in~ (_fast16_files) or FolderPath has @"fast16" or RegistryValueName has "SvcMgmt" or RegistryValueData has "svcmgmt.exe" or RegistryValueData has "fast16.sys"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, EventSource=ActionType, Detail=strcat(coalesce(FolderPath,""), "\\", coalesce(FileName,""), " reg=", coalesce(RegistryValueData,"")), SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName ),
  ( DeviceRegistryEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType in ("RegistryValueSet","RegistryKeyCreated")
    | where (RegistryKey has @"\Services\SvcMgmt")
         or (RegistryKey has @"\Session Manager\Memory Management\PrefetchParameters" and RegistryValueName =~ "EnablePrefetcher" and RegistryValueData == "0")
         or (RegistryValueData has "svcmgmt.exe" or RegistryValueData has "fast16.sys")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, EventSource=strcat("Registry:", ActionType), Detail=strcat(RegistryKey, " ", RegistryValueName, "=", RegistryValueData), SHA256=InitiatingProcessSHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName )
| summarize HitCount=count(), FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp), Sources=make_set(EventSource), Details=make_set(Detail, 20) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| where HitCount >= 1
| order by LastSeen desc
let _fast16_files = dynamic(["svcmgmt.exe","svcmgmt.dll","fast16.sys"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  ( ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
    | where TargetFileName in~ (_fast16_files) or TargetFilePath has @"\Device\fast16"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer=DvcHostname, EventSource=strcat("File:", EventType), Detail=TargetFilePath, ActingProcessName, ActorUsername ),
  ( ImRegistryEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where (RegistryKey has @"\Services\SvcMgmt")
         or (RegistryKey has @"\Session Manager\Memory Management\PrefetchParameters" and RegistryValue =~ "EnablePrefetcher" and tostring(RegistryValueData) == "0")
         or (tostring(RegistryValueData) has "svcmgmt.exe" or tostring(RegistryValueData) has "fast16.sys")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer=DvcHostname, EventSource="Registry", Detail=strcat(RegistryKey, " ", RegistryValue, "=", tostring(RegistryValueData)), ActingProcessName, ActorUsername ),
  ( SecurityEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where EventID == 4697
    | where ServiceName has "SvcMgmt" or ServiceFileName has "svcmgmt.exe" or ServiceFileName has "fast16.sys"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, EventSource="ServiceInstalled-4697", Detail=strcat(ServiceName, " -> ", ServiceFileName, " start=", ServiceStartType), ActingProcessName=ProcessName, ActorUsername=SubjectUserName ),
  ( WindowsEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Provider == "Microsoft-Windows-Sysmon"

    | where PipeName has "p577" or PipeName has @"\\.\pipe\p577"

  ( WindowsEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Provider == "Microsoft-Windows-Sysmon"
    | where EventID == 6
    | extend ImageLoaded = tostring(EventData.ImageLoaded), Hashes = tostring(EventData.Hashes)
    | where ImageLoaded has "fast16" or ImageLoaded has "svcmgmt"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, EventSource="DriverLoad-Sysmon6", Detail=strcat(ImageLoaded, " ", Hashes), ActingProcessName=ImageLoaded, ActorUsername="" )
| summarize HitCount=count(), FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated), Sources=make_set(EventSource), Details=make_set(Detail, 20) by Computer, ActingProcessName, ActorUsername
| where HitCount >= 1
| order by LastSeen desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449") or SHA1 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449") or MD5 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449") or SHA1 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449") or MD5 in~ ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449")

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

        where Processes.process_hash IN ("9a10e1faa86a5d39417cae44da5adf38824dfb9a16432e34df766aa1dc9e3525", "07c69fc33271cf5a2ce03ac1fed7a3b16357aec093c5bf9ef61fbfa4348d0529", "8fcb4d3d4df61719ee3da98241393779290e0efcd88a49e363e2a2dfbc04dae9", "de584703c78a60a56028f9834086facd1401b355", "dbe51eabebf9d4ef9581ef99844a2944", "0ff6abe0252d4f37a196a1231fae5f26", "410eddfc19de44249897986ecc8ac449")
        by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process_hash

ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002 T1566
Click any ATT&CK pill below to open it on the Matrix

Phase 6
Command & Control

Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Office Application Spawning Script Host
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d07
status: experimental
description: |
    Word/Excel/Outlook/OneNote spawning powershell, cmd, wscript,
    cscript, mshta, rundll32, regsvr32 — classic macro / embedded-
    object payload signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1566/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059
    - attack.t1566.001
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\winword.exe'
            - '\excel.exe'
            - '\powerpnt.exe'
            - '\outlook.exe'
            - '\onenote.exe'
            - '\mspub.exe'
            - '\visio.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate automation macros in trusted internal documents
level: high

title: Remote-Management (RMM) Tool Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0c
status: experimental

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:

      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

Is Shai-Hulud Back? Compromised Bitwarden CLI Contains a Self-Propagating npm Worm

Aikido
2026-04-23 11 use cases 5 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

Malware found in @bitwarden/cli v2026.4.0 steals SSH keys, cloud secrets, and AI coding tool credentials, then spreads through victims' own npm packages. Inside: a worm calling itself "Shai-Hulud: The Third Coming." Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

ATT&CKT1027 T1176 T1190 T1195.002 T1555.003
Domainsaudit.checkmarx.cx
SHA25637f34aa3b86db689… 18f784b3bc9a0bcd…

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Is Shai-Hulud Back? Compromised Bitwarden CLI Contains a Self-Propagating npm Wo
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or SHA1 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or MD5 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or SHA1 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or MD5 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.file_hash IN ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name, Filesystem.file_hash
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| append
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
        where Processes.process_hash IN ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
        by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process_hash
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`]

Can AI Attack the Cloud? Lessons From Building an Autonomous Cloud Offensive Multi-Agent System

Unit 42 (Palo Alto)
2026-04-23 10 use cases 12 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

Unit 42 reveals how multi-agent AI systems can autonomously attack cloud environments. Learn critical insights and vital lessons for proactive security. The post Can AI Attack the Cloud? Lessons From Building an Autonomous Cloud Offensive Multi-Agent System appeared first on Unit 42 .

ATT&CKT1069 T1078.004 T1087 T1190 T1199 T1526 T1537 T1548.005 T1552.005 T1566 T1580 T1619

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Compute Engine IMDS credential fetch from web-application runtime (SSRF chain entry) · Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Burst GCP IAM/discovery enumeration by a single service account (AI-agent velocity fingerprint) · [LLM] GCP service-account impersonation chain via iamcredentials.GenerateAccessToken / SignJwt · [LLM] BigQuery query results or table data exported to a foreign GCP project / external GCS bucket · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] Burst GCP IAM/discovery enumeration by a single service account (AI-agent velocity fingerprint) Actions on Objectives Medium
Detects the machine-speed enumeration pattern characteristic of an autonomous LLM agent (Zealot-style) operating with a stolen IMDS service-account token. A single service-account principal calls 10+ distinct IAM, resource-list and policy-introspection methods within 60 seconds — well above the cadence of any normal CI/CD or Terraform run. Rationale: Article explicitly highlights AI-agent operations occurring at 'machine speed' that 'no human team could match' and lists Cloud Infrastructure Discovery (T1580) + Cloud Service Discovery (T1526) + IAM enumeration sequence as the alert table's discovery-phase signal. The 10-distinct-methods/60s threshold is the distinguishing feature between autonomous-agent enumeration and normal IaC tooling. Corroborated against MITRE ATT&CK T1087/T1526. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1526/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1580/
ATT&CKT1087 T1069 T1526 T1580
Data sourcesChange
CloudAppEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where Application == "Google Cloud Platform"
| where AccountDisplayName endswith ".iam.gserviceaccount.com"
| where ActionType has_any ("testIamPermissions","getIamPolicy","ListServiceAccounts","Roles.list","projects.list","compute.instances.list","storage.buckets.list","bigquery.datasets.list","secretmanager.secrets.list","serviceusage.services.list")
| summarize DistinctActions = dcount(ActionType), Actions = make_set(ActionType), Calls = count(), FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp)
    by AccountDisplayName, IPAddress, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where DistinctActions >= 10  // empirical AI-agent fan-out threshold from Zealot PoC
| order by FirstSeen desc
source:gcp.audit @protoPayload.authenticationInfo.principalEmail:*.iam.gserviceaccount.com @protoPayload.methodName:(*testIamPermissions OR *getIamPolicy OR *ListServiceAccounts OR projects.list OR roles.list OR compute.instances.list OR storage.buckets.list OR bigquery.datasets.list OR secretmanager.secrets.list OR serviceusage.services.list)
CLOUD
index=gcp_audit (sourcetype="google:gcp:pubsub:message" OR sourcetype="gcp:audit")
| spath protoPayload.methodName output=method
| spath protoPayload.authenticationInfo.principalEmail output=principal
| where like(principal,"%.iam.gserviceaccount.com") AND (match(method,"(?i)(testIamPermissions|getIamPolicy|listServiceAccounts|projects\.list|roles\.list|compute\.instances\.list|storage\.buckets\.list|bigquery\.datasets\.list|secretmanager\.secrets\.list|serviceusage\.services\.list)"))
| bin _time span=60s
| stats dc(method) as DistinctMethods, values(method) as Methods, count as Calls by _time, principal
| where DistinctMethods >= 10
| `drop_dm_object_name(Change)`
[LLM] GCP service-account impersonation chain via iamcredentials.GenerateAccessToken / SignJwt Actions on Objectives High
Detects the privilege-escalation step the Zealot PoC walks after IMDS credential theft: calling `iamcredentials.googleapis.com.GenerateAccessToken` (or SignJwt / SignBlob / GenerateIdToken) to mint a token for a different, higher-privileged service account. Flags impersonation events where the actor→target SA pair has never been observed in the prior 30-day baseline. Rationale: The alert table calls out 'GCP service account impersonation attempt' mapped to T1078.004 + T1548 — this is the exact API surface the Zealot supervisor agent uses to escalate from compromised SA to org-wide access. iamcredentials.googleapis.com.GenerateAccessToken is a high-fidelity API call (rare in benign workloads outside workload-identity-federation); baselining the actor→target pair reduces FPs to near-zero. Cross-checked against: • https://cloud.google.com/iam/docs/service-account-impersonation • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1078/004/
ATT&CKT1078.004 T1548
Data sourcesChange
let Baseline = CloudAppEvents
    | where Timestamp between (ago(30d) .. ago(1d))
    | where Application == "Google Cloud Platform"
    | where ActionType in ("GenerateAccessToken","SignJwt","SignBlob","GenerateIdToken")
    | extend Raw = parse_json(RawEventData)
    | extend TargetSa = tostring(Raw.protoPayload.request.name)
    | extend Pair = strcat(AccountDisplayName, " -> ", TargetSa)
    | summarize by Pair;
CloudAppEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where Application == "Google Cloud Platform"
| where ActionType in ("GenerateAccessToken","SignJwt","SignBlob","GenerateIdToken")
| extend Raw = parse_json(RawEventData)
| extend TargetSa = tostring(Raw.protoPayload.request.name)
| extend Pair = strcat(AccountDisplayName, " -> ", TargetSa)
| join kind=leftanti Baseline on Pair
| project Timestamp, Actor=AccountDisplayName, TargetSa, IPAddress, ActionType, RawEventData
| order by Timestamp desc
title: GCP Service Account Impersonation via IAM Credentials API
id: 7c1f3a8b-2e44-4f1c-a3d5-9c8b4e1f2a3b
status: experimental
description: Detects service-account impersonation through GenerateAccessToken, SignJwt, SignBlob, or GenerateIdToken on iamcredentials.googleapis.com — the privilege-escalation primitive used by autonomous offensive agents (Zealot PoC) after IMDS credential theft.
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/autonomous-ai-cloud-attacks/
  - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1078/004/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/19
tags:
  - attack.privilege_escalation
  - attack.t1078.004
  - attack.t1548
logsource:
  product: gcp
  service: gcp.audit
detection:
  selection:
    data.protoPayload.serviceName: 'iamcredentials.googleapis.com'
    data.protoPayload.methodName:
      - 'GenerateAccessToken'
      - 'SignJwt'
      - 'SignBlob'
      - 'GenerateIdToken'
  condition: selection
falsepositives:
  - Legitimate workload identity federation in GKE / Cloud Run
  - Admins using gcloud --impersonate-service-account for break-glass
level: medium

source:gcp.audit @protoPayload.serviceName:iamcredentials.googleapis.com @protoPayload.methodName:(GenerateAccessToken OR SignJwt OR SignBlob OR GenerateIdToken)
CLOUD
index=gcp_audit protoPayload.serviceName="iamcredentials.googleapis.com" (protoPayload.methodName="GenerateAccessToken" OR protoPayload.methodName="SignJwt" OR protoPayload.methodName="SignBlob" OR protoPayload.methodName="GenerateIdToken")
| spath protoPayload.authenticationInfo.principalEmail output=actor
| spath protoPayload.request.name output=target_sa
| rex field=target_sa "projects/-/serviceAccounts/(?<target_email>[^/]+)"
| eval pair=actor." -> ".target_email
| stats earliest(_time) as first_seen, latest(_time) as last_seen, count by pair, actor, target_email
| join type=left pair [ search index=gcp_audit earliest=-30d@d latest=-1d@d protoPayload.serviceName="iamcredentials.googleapis.com"
    | spath protoPayload.authenticationInfo.principalEmail output=actor
    | spath protoPayload.request.name output=target_sa
    | rex field=target_sa "projects/-/serviceAccounts/(?<target_email>[^/]+)"
    | eval pair=actor." -> ".target_email
    | stats count as baseline_count by pair ]
| where isnull(baseline_count)
| `drop_dm_object_name(Change)`
[LLM] BigQuery query results or table data exported to a foreign GCP project / external GCS bucket Actions on Objectives High
Detects the data-exfiltration end-state Zealot demonstrates: BigQuery jobs whose destination table, GCS extract target, or query-result destination resides in a project / bucket outside the org's allow-list. Pairs `bigquery.jobs.create` and `bigquery.tables.export` audit events with cross-project destination paths. Rationale: The alert table explicitly calls out 'BigQuery table or query results exfiltrated to a foreign project' (T1537) — this was the terminal stage of the Zealot attack chain in the article. Detecting destination projectId/URI mismatches against the source project (and an allow-list of legitimate cross-project data shares) gives a near-zero-FP exfiltration signal because legitimate cross-project pipelines are stable and few in number. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1537/ • https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/exporting-data
ATT&CKT1537 T1567.002
Data sourcesWeb
let AllowedProjects = dynamic(["org-prod-data","org-analytics-prod","org-shared-bigquery"]);  // populate from CMDB
CloudAppEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where Application == "Google Cloud Platform"
| where ActionType has_any ("InsertJob","jobservice.jobcompleted","tables.export","tabledata.list")
| extend Raw = parse_json(RawEventData)
| extend SrcProject = tostring(Raw.resource.labels.project_id)
| extend DestQueryProject = tostring(Raw.protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.queryConfig.destinationTable.projectId)
| extend ExtractUris = tostring(Raw.protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.extractConfig.destinationUris)
| where (isnotempty(DestQueryProject) and DestQueryProject != SrcProject and DestQueryProject !in (AllowedProjects))
     or (isnotempty(ExtractUris) and not(ExtractUris has_any (AllowedProjects)))
| project Timestamp, Principal=AccountDisplayName, IPAddress, SrcProject, DestQueryProject, ExtractUris, ActionType
| order by Timestamp desc
source:gcp.audit @protoPayload.serviceName:bigquery.googleapis.com @protoPayload.methodName:(google.cloud.bigquery.v2.JobService.InsertJob OR jobservice.jobcompleted OR jobservice.insert) (@protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.queryConfig.destinationTable.projectId:* OR @protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.extractConfig.destinationUris:*)
CLOUD
index=gcp_audit protoPayload.serviceName="bigquery.googleapis.com" (protoPayload.methodName="google.cloud.bigquery.v2.JobService.InsertJob" OR protoPayload.methodName="jobservice.jobcompleted" OR protoPayload.methodName="jobservice.insert")
| spath resource.labels.project_id output=src_project
| spath protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.queryConfig.destinationTable.projectId output=dest_query_project
| spath protoPayload.metadata.jobChange.job.jobConfig.extractConfig.destinationUris{} output=extract_uris
| spath protoPayload.authenticationInfo.principalEmail output=principal
| eval dest_extract_project=if(isnotnull(extract_uris), mvfilter(match(extract_uris,"gs://[^/]+")), null())
| where (isnotnull(dest_query_project) AND dest_query_project!=src_project) OR isnotnull(extract_uris)
| stats values(dest_query_project) as ForeignDestProject, values(extract_uris) as ExtractTargets, count by _time, src_project, principal
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
[LLM] Compute Engine IMDS credential fetch from web-application runtime (SSRF chain entry) Exploitation High
Detects the SSRF→IMDS step the Zealot PoC starts its chain with: a web-application process (nginx, apache, node, python, java, php-fpm, ruby) reaching 169.254.169.254 or metadata.google.internal on the GCE instance to fetch a service-account token. Pivots on the parent-process binary to separate legitimate metadata access (cloud-init, ops-agent) from app-runtime access driven by an SSRF payload. Rationale: Article describes the Zealot chain as starting with SSRF exploitation followed by IMDS credential theft (alert-table row 'Cloud Unusual Instance Metadata Service (IMDS) access' mapped to T1552.005). The 169.254.169.254 IP is the IMDS endpoint on GCE; pivoting on the initiating process binary (web runtime vs system agent) is the noise-killer because legitimate IMDS calls come from cloud-init / ops-agent, never from apache/nginx/python. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1552/005/ • https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/metadata/overview
ATT&CKT1552.005 T1190
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Endpoint.Processes
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP == "169.254.169.254" or RemoteUrl has "metadata.google.internal"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("nginx","apache2","httpd","node","node.js","python","python3","java","php-fpm","php","gunicorn","uwsgi","tomcat","ruby","puma")
| where InitiatingProcessParentFileName !in~ ("systemd","cloud-init","google_metadata_script_runner","google_osconfig_agent")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, LocalIP
| order by Timestamp desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr == "169.254.169.254" or DstHostname has "metadata.google.internal"
| join kind=leftouter ImProcessCreate on $left.SrcHostname == $right.DvcHostname
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("nginx","apache2","httpd","node","python","python3","java","php-fpm","gunicorn","uwsgi","tomcat","ruby","puma")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname=SrcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: GCP Compute Engine IMDS Access from Web Application Runtime (SSRF Chain)
id: a4b3c2d1-1234-5678-9abc-def012345678
status: experimental
description: Detects a web-application process (nginx, apache, node, python, java, php-fpm, ruby) connecting to the GCP metadata service (169.254.169.254 / metadata.google.internal), indicative of SSRF-driven service-account token theft as demonstrated by the Unit 42 Zealot PoC.
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/autonomous-ai-cloud-attacks/
  - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1552/005/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/19
tags:
  - attack.credential_access
  - attack.t1552.005
  - attack.t1190
logsource:
  category: network_connection
  product: linux
detection:
  selection_net:
    DestinationIp: '169.254.169.254'
  selection_image:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/nginx'
      - '/apache2'
      - '/httpd'
      - '/node'
      - '/python'
      - '/python3'
      - '/java'
      - '/php-fpm'
      - '/gunicorn'
      - '/uwsgi'
      - '/tomcat'
      - '/ruby'
      - '/puma'
  condition: selection_net and selection_image
falsepositives:
  - cloud-init and google_metadata_script_runner legitimately querying IMDS at boot
  - Ops Agent / OS Config Agent periodic metadata polling
level: high

(source:linux.auditd OR source:linux.syslog OR source:windows.sysmon) (@network.destination.ip:169.254.169.254 OR @DestinationIp:169.254.169.254 OR @dns.question.name:metadata.google.internal) @process.name:(nginx OR apache2 OR httpd OR node OR python OR python3 OR java OR php-fpm OR gunicorn OR uwsgi OR tomcat OR ruby OR puma) -@process.executable.path:(/usr/bin/google_metadata_script_runner OR /usr/bin/google_osconfig_agent OR /usr/lib/google-cloud-sdk/*)
#event_simpleName=NetworkConnectIP4 RemoteAddressIP4="169.254.169.254"
| ContextImageFileName=/(nginx|apache2|httpd|node|python(3)?|java|php-fpm|gunicorn|uwsgi|tomcat|ruby|puma)$/i
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/(google_metadata_script_runner|google_osconfig_agent|cloud-init)/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, RemoteAddressIP4], function=count())
| count > 0

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
